# The OpenConditions data model

`@openconditions/model` (Apache-2.0) is the typed model every OpenConditions
record follows, and the registry that closes its taxonomy. It has no
dependency on any other OpenConditions package: storage, ingest, publishers,
federation and third parties all build on it.

## Records

Every record shares one kernel — id, class, kind/type/subtype, domain,
temporality, location, provenance, freshness, validity, relations — and is one
of four classes:

| class         | what it is                                                                                                 |
| ------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `feature`     | a persistent thing with a lifecycle (a charger, a car park, a gauge), with addressable components          |
| `situation`   | a bounded-in-time condition: `type` is its nature, `causes[]` why, `effects[]` what it does to travel      |
| `observation` | a timestamped result for a registered property about a feature, component, segments, location or situation |
| `offer`       | a structured tariff on a feature or component                                                              |

Record ids are `oc:<class>:<namespace>:<localId>`. The namespace is a source id
(`[a-z0-9-]+`) or, for records an instance originates, its instance id (a
hostname works); it never contains `:`. `canonicalId` is
`sha256([namespace, localId])`.

`Effect` is the single routing and impact contract: closures, lane
restrictions, speed and dimension limits, access rules, hazmat rules, detours,
contraflow, advisories and — for a restriction a parser recognised but could
not type — `unsupported`. An effect whose applicability is `unknown` or whose
normalization is not `complete` is restriction evidence: stored and listed,
withheld from shared routing.

## Registry

Domain packages contribute registry modules; one `buildRegistry([...])` call
assembles them with the kernel module into a closed registry and fails at
startup on any inconsistency (unknown domain or vocabulary, duplicate code,
closed vocabulary extended, malformed version).

```ts
import { buildRegistry, defineKind, kernelModule } from "@openconditions/model";
import { z } from "zod";

const registry = buildRegistry([
  kernelModule,
  {
    name: "roads",
    entries: [
      defineKind({
        class: "situation",
        code: "incident",
        domain: "roads",
        version: "1.0",
        description: "Unplanned events on the road",
        types: { accident: ["multi_vehicle", "overturned"] },
        details: () => ({ vehiclesInvolved: z.number().int().optional() }),
      }),
    ],
  },
]);

registry.validateDraft(parserOutput); // derived fields must be absent
registry.validate(storedRecord); // derived fields must be present and consistent
```

- `defineDomain`, `defineVocabulary` / `extendVocabulary` — closed and
  registry-extensible value lists (extensible ones grow with the modules that
  extend them; the assembled list is a closed enum).
- `defineKind` — feature, component, situation and offer kinds, with their
  closed type/subtype lists and `details` schema.
- `defineProperty` — observation properties: result form, canonical unit or
  vocabulary, allowed subjects, qualifiers, retention and fusion metadata.
- `defineEffect`, `defineSelector`, `defineResultSchema` — effect variants,
  `Situation.affects` keys and structured observation results.

Validation is hard: every object is closed, controlled values come only from
registered vocabularies, and dispatch is by `(class, kind | property, v)`.

The packages are layered so the assembled registry never depends on parsers or
storage:

| package                          | holds                                                                                       | depends on                 |
| -------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------- |
| `@openconditions/model`          | kernel and registry framework                                                               | no OpenConditions package  |
| `@openconditions/model-<domain>` | one domain's registry module: kinds, properties, effects, vocabulary extensions, crosswalks | `model` only               |
| `@openconditions/model-registry` | `productionRegistry()`: the kernel plus every domain module, and the published JSON Schemas | `model` and `model-*` only |

Services, publishers and federation validate and seal with
`productionRegistry()`; storage and the domain parser packages never import it.
The database CHECKs only vocabularies the kernel closes itself (fuzziness,
privacy class, evidence state), built from the model's constants; columns whose
values domain modules contribute (kinds, properties, domains, source formats,
effect kinds) are guarded by the write seam, not by a constraint, because a
registry-driven CHECK would make every new property a table-locking migration.
Instead each service fails at boot when the database holds a code the running
registry does not register (`assertStoredCodesRegistered`). Only ingest checks
the formats of the loaded sources, after syncing them: a service that reads no
feed would otherwise fail its boot on a format a release retired until ingest
had marked its source inactive.

Crosswalks to external vocabularies live on the entries: `mappings` on a kind
or a property, `typeMappings` per `type` or `type.subtype`, `valueMappings` per
vocabulary value. `registry.crosswalk` resolves them both ways — a parser's
source code to a situation or feature classification, a property (DATEX
`TrafficSpeed/averageVehicleSpeed` → `traffic.speed`) or a vocabulary value,
and back to an emitter's code. A code of a format OpenConditions parses
(DATEX II, WZDx, Open511, IBI 511) must resolve to one target; emitter codes
(TraFF, GTFS-RT, Road511) may be shared. Domain modules write crosswalks as
tables (`withSituationCrosswalks`, `withFeatureCrosswalks`,
`withPropertyCrosswalks`, `vocabularyCrosswalk`), so a table stays the single
source of truth and the entries carry what it says.

A property names its subjects by feature kind, by component kind, or by
trait: `device.status` (roads) applies to every kind with the `field_device`
trait, including the weather module's `weather_station`, without either module
importing the other. Blocks several domains share live in the kernel: the
`sensor_channel` component (one measured value stream of a site), the
`surface_state` vocabulary and the traits.

A property's `retention` says what history its series keeps: every distinct
result or only changes (`changeOnly`), raw rows for `rawDays`, an hourly or
daily rollup of numeric results (an hourly histogram with a bin width, for
percentiles over long windows), or only the latest value (`latestOnly`). The
registry rejects a retention the result type cannot have.

A change-only property is also written as its changes: a reading whose result
(and qualifiers) is the one its series holds is not written at all, so the
series keeps the time its result was first stated. A bulk feed's reading of
such a property therefore stores no `validUntil`; its validity is computed
when it is read: `validUntil = end of its source's last successful poll +
max(30 min, 2 × the source's shortest data cadence)` (`withPolledValidity`).
A feed that keeps polling keeps its unchanged readings current, one that
stops goes stale. A reading a peer sent holds from when this instance last
received its source from that peer (`federation_source_receipt`) by the same
window. A fused reading of a change-only property is valid as long as the
latest of its contributing feeds, and the fusions of a source whose freshness
flips are refreshed by a scheduled job; in a series' history a reading holds
until the next change. A full poll that states every reading of its source
ends the series it no longer states (a charge point removed, a site
withdrawn): their reading takes `validUntil` = that poll, until a later poll
states them again. Crowd readings keep their own lifetimes, on-demand answers
their expiry, and other properties the `validUntil` their source states.
Readings carry the publisher's time where it gives one; where it gives none,
the fetch that first saw the result. A state older than the one in effect,
and a restated result, are not written; a reading whose site the poll changed
is.

An observation's id is derived, not chosen: `observationId(namespace, o)`
hashes its series (subject key, property, qualifier key) and its point (the
phenomenon start in UTC, plus the issue time for a forecast), and validation
rejects any other id. Two spellings of one instant are one observation.

`defineChangeKind` names a kind of change between two revisions; the kernel's
change kinds watch every content field, and `computeChangeKinds(registry,
previous, next)` lists the ones a new revision carries (`created`,
`tombstoned`, or e.g. `["severity_change", "effects_change"]`).

An effect's state is evaluated at the edge, never stored:
`effectStateAt(effect, situationValidity, at)` gives `active`, `scheduled`,
`ended` or `unknown` plus the next transition, and `routingBlockers(effect)`
lists why it may not constrain shared routing (vehicles unknown, not fully
normalized, an operator action that is only requested).

## Versioning

Every entry has a `major.minor` version; the wire carries the major (`v` on
`details`, on each effect and on structured results). Adding an optional field
bumps the minor; removing, renaming or retyping a field bumps the major. An
instance validates only the current major of each entry. The kernel itself is
versioned too (`kernel@1.0`) and negotiated at federation like a kind.

## Drafts and stored records

Parsers produce drafts. The write seam (`sealRecord`) derives `canonicalId`,
`domain`, `provenance.instanceId` and `contentHash`; storage assigns `revision`
and `recordedAt`. A draft that asserts a derived field is rejected.

The content hash covers content only. Which record keys count as content is
declared per class in a map the typechecker holds complete: a new field cannot
be added to a class without deciding whether it changes a record's revision.
Freshness, identity, evidence, OSM matching, the raw payload reference and
provenance hop metadata never do.

## Published artifacts

`pnpm gen:model` writes the JSON Schema of every registry entry to
`packages/model-registry/schemas/` (`kernel@1.json` holds the kernel `$defs`; each
kind, property, effect, selector and result schema has its own file, listed in
`index.json`) and regenerates the section below. `pnpm check:model` and the
artifacts test fail when either is stale.

## Roads

`@openconditions/model-roads` registers the `roads` domain: fifteen situation
kinds (`incident`, `roadworks`, `closure`, `restriction`, `weather_condition`,
`road_condition`, `road_hazard`, `public_event`, `authority`,
`equipment_fault`, `security`, `winter_operation`, `pass_status`,
`congestion`, `other`), every roads wire format, the `lanes_change` change
kind and a severity rule per kind.

Crosswalk tables are the single source of truth: one table per source
standard maps its codes to `kind.type[.subtype]` (DATEX II v2.3 and v3 record
class and discriminator value, WZDx event, work and restriction types, Open511
event types and subtypes, IBI 511 event types), one per emitter maps our
classifications to its codes (TraFF events, GTFS-RT causes, Road511 types).
The module derives the kinds' `typeMappings` from them. Coverage tests hold
every table against the standard's own value list (vendored verbatim from the
official schemas): every source value is mapped or explicitly `null`, and every
registered type has an emitter code or an explicit `null`. The DATEX II export
picks its record class from a table of its own, `DATEX2_RECORDS`
(`crosswalk/emitters.ts`), because several DATEX codes read in as one type and
the inbound table cannot say which one to write
([DATEX II export](datex-conformance.md)). Provider vocabularies with no
standard behind them (Digitraffic, LTA, Autobahn and the like) map through plain tables
in `crosswalk/providers.ts`: no emitter writes them.

Each roads event parser reads its format into a parse record that stays inside
`@openconditions/roads`, with the classification from those crosswalks, the
publisher's own record time and whether the headline came from the source.
`parseEvents(feed, payloads, { fetchedAt })` turns one poll into situation
drafts (a `ParseOutput`, defined in `@openconditions/ingest-framework`), dated
by the poll. For a source that declares a complete snapshot it also accounts
for every input record, naming the situations of records it could not place so
the poll cannot end them. The assembly follows these rules:

- A DATEX situation's records fold into one situation. A record that names a
  nature (an accident) is the situation; lane, speed, rerouting and winter
  management records become its effects, each keeping `sourceRecordRef`, and
  its own validity and geometry when they differ. Records of different natures
  split into situations sharing `groupId`.
- When no record names a nature, the stated cause does: NDW publishes
  roadworks as lane-management records caused by `roadMaintenance`.
- A detour joins the situation it serves when the snapshot holds it; a detour
  without its parent becomes a `closure.closure` situation marked `derived`.
- A severity the source declares is kept; otherwise the kind's rule derives
  one from the effects (a closure is major, lanes by the share closed, a delay
  of 20 minutes or more at least major), or it stays `unknown`.
- A restriction from a parser without the restriction contract is kept as
  `partial` evidence (a `dimension_limit` in canonical units, an `access`
  rule, or an `unsupported` carrier), so it is listed but never routes.

Golden files under `packages/roads/src/__tests__/golden/` hold the sealed
situations `parseEvents` drafts from the reviewed fixtures of the roads
domain's `situations` formats. The drafts keep multilingual text with each value's language (Open511 and IBI 511
payloads declare no language, so their text is tagged `und`), a declared
severity's verbatim token (`severity.declaredRaw`), and a DATEX one-off
validity period with its exact start and end.

## Road infrastructure

Three modules register the things along the road and what they measure:

- `@openconditions/model-roads` adds the feature kinds `measurement_site`,
  `vms` and `camera`, their components (`sign_face`, `camera_view`, and the
  kernel's `sensor_channel`), the `traffic.*` properties, `vms.display`
  (`vms_display@1`), `camera.image` (`camera_image@1`) and `device.status`.
- `@openconditions/model-weather` registers the `weather` domain: the
  `weather_station` kind and the measured and forecast `weather.*` and `road.*`
  properties. Salt concentration is mass per volume (`kg/m3`), as every source
  publishes it; wind direction keeps no hourly mean (bearings do not average).
- `@openconditions/model-vehicles` registers the `vehicles` domain:
  `service_vehicle` and `vehicle.position` (`vehicle_position@1`). A vehicle
  has no fixed place, so its feature carries no geometry; each position
  observation is located where it was reported.

Crosswalk tables cover DATEX II `vmsType`, sign working status, device health,
traffic status, precipitation type and road-surface condition type, the DATEX
II measured values of both versions (every value of every measured-data class
maps to a property or is marked `null`), and the WZDx device types and
statuses. Coverage tests hold them against the vendored value lists.

The flow parsers return drafts: `parseFlows` (and the streaming
`measuredDataReader` for DATEX measured data) turns one payload into
`measurement_site` features, `traffic.*` observations and the congestion
situations derived from them. A site's lanes and vehicle classes are
`sensor_channel` components with readings of their own; volumes, occupancy,
per-class speed vectors and measurement periods (`phenomenonTime` intervals)
are kept where the source states them, and site tables and station registries
give sites their names, lane counts, equipment and per-index lane, class and
period. DATEX lanes are converted to the model's left-first numbering with the
site's lane count. A level of service OpenConditions computed from speed and
free-flow speed stays on the speed's `baseline` instead of becoming a reading
of the source (`enrichReadings` applies stored baselines the same way), and the
congestion situations derived from flow point at their site (`derivedFrom`)
and carry no invented headline. Golden files hold the sealed records of every
flow format's fixture, and pin the streaming reader to the buffered parse.

A fit check (`infrastructure-fit.test.ts`) maps real records of every kind
onto the production registry: a Digitraffic road-weather station and weather
camera, Digitraffic variable signs, NDW route-information panels (DATEX II 3),
WSDOT arrow boards (WZDx 4.2), Ontario 511 cameras, a Digitraffic traffic
station (DATEX II 3.7 site table and measured data) and Iowa DOT snowplows.
The two camera sources run through the cameras domain's parsers
(`packages/cameras`) on their catalogue feeds. What it found:

- Numbered surface sensors (`TIE_1`, `TIE_2`) are separate series, so each is
  a `sensor_channel` of its station.
- Route panels mostly show bitmaps: a message can be a `graphic`, whose bytes
  stay in the archived raw payload.
- Arrow-board patterns are WZDx codes (`codeSystem: "wzdx"`), and a simple
  DATEX matrix sign is a pictogram sign, not lane control.
- A Digitraffic station is one camera whose presets are its views; each
  preset's still is a `camera.image` reading keyed by the preset, dated by the
  publisher's image time, `online` while that time is within three collection
  intervals.
- Ontario publishes no image time and no licence for its images, so they are
  `imageRedistribution: "unknown"` with status `unknown`; "Enabled" says a view
  is configured, not that it delivers.
- Digitraffic's DATEX site table carries national-grid coordinates under WGS84
  element names; positions come from the station metadata.
- Digitraffic's snowplow tracking has no vehicle identity, so it does not fit
  `service_vehicle`; Iowa's AVL feed, which names its trucks, does.

## Facilities

Three modules register the places a journey stops at, and the first offers:

- `@openconditions/model-parking` registers the `parking` domain:
  `parking_site` (off-street, on-street, park and ride, lorry parking, rest
  area), its `parking_area` and `parking_space` components, the occupancy
  properties `parking.available`, `parking.occupied`, `parking.occupancy_pct`,
  `parking.status` and `parking.trend`, and the `parking_rate` offer. An area
  is a component rather than a vector result because each has its own
  capacity, dimensions and occupancy series.
- `@openconditions/model-charging` registers the `charging` domain: one
  `charging_site` per OCPI location, with an `evse` component per charge point
  and a `connector` component below it per plug, the statuses
  `charging.evse_status`, `charging.connector_status`, `charging.site_status`
  and `charging.waiting_time`, and the `energy_tariff` offer.
- `@openconditions/model-fuel` registers the `fuel` domain: `fuel_station`
  with one `fuel_product` component per priced product (grade × service level ×
  price level × sale unit), `fuel.price`, `fuel.price_cap` and
  `fuel.product_available`. A price is an observation, not an offer: sources
  publish one scalar per product with a timestamp. A regional average has no
  station to hang on, so its subject is the administrative area and the grade
  moves into the qualifiers; the district scheme US averages use (`padd`) is
  registered with the domain.

What a site offers besides parking, charging or fuelling is one shared
`amenity` vocabulary in the kernel, because every kind of site publishes the
same shop, shower or toilet, under DATEX II service-facility and equipment
types or OCPI facilities.

Crosswalks cover OCPI 2.2.1 (EVSE status, connector type and format, power
type, facility, parking type, tariff type and dimension, image category),
DATEX II v3 energy infrastructure (refill-point status, connector type and
format, site type) and parking (usage scenario, structure type, opening,
operation and place status, occupancy trend, security), DATEX II v2 parking
(site status, vacant spaces, overcrowding, usage scenario, service facility
and equipment types), the DATEX fuel grades of both versions, and ParkAPI v3's
type and purpose pair. Coverage tests hold every table against the vendored
value lists.

### Identity linking

Two sources publishing the same site are recognised by `proposeLink`, which
implements the three tiers: a shared id of a scheme the kind declares
authoritative, a conservative spatial match, or nothing. A spatial match needs
both positions to be exactly known, the types to be compatible (a kerbside
stretch is never a garage), and either a distance below the kind's close
window or a name, operator or address that agrees; a weaker pair is returned
as `pending` for a human instead of linked. A kind may bound how far one
attribute links on its own (`attributeWithinMetres`): a charging operator's
name alone links only within 50 m, since a large operator runs sites a street
apart. Two addresses with house numbers that cannot be one house (`2a` and
`2b`, `53` and `49-51`; a missing letter or a number inside a range is one
house, and a number that names the street, `B 3`, is none) never agree.
Conflicting ids of one scheme block a link outright. `canonicalClusters` turns the accepted links into the
canonical view, where every feature has a cluster and an unlinked feature is a
cluster of one; two clusters merge only when every pair across them links, so
a row of neighbouring charge points never chains into one blob. `matchOsm`
finds the OSM element a feature is, either because one side asserts the
other's id (`ref:EU:EVSE`, which holds every charge point of a site separated
by semicolons) or by the same spatial rules against elements tagged as that
kind.

### Access modes

A source is polled in bulk or read on demand for the area a consumer asks
about. An on-demand record must say when its answer goes stale
(`freshness.expiresAt`), and `federationEligible` and `historyEligible` keep
it out of the federation outbox, the archive, the observation partitions and
the revision tables: many instances exchanging each other's on-demand answers
would rebuild exactly the collection those publishers' terms forbid.

### Facilities fit check

`facilities-fit.test.ts` maps real records onto the production registry:
charging sites and tariffs from the MobiData BW charge-point database (OCPI
2.2), parking sites from MobiData BW ParkAPI v3, the NDW lorry-park table and
status, Autobahn GmbH lorry parking, Spanish MINETUR and Italian MIMIT fuel
prices, and Austrian E-Control prices as an on-demand source. The charge-point
database, ParkAPI, NDW, MINETUR and E-Control run through the charging, parking
and fuel domains' own parsers.
`linking-fit.test.ts` runs the linking rules over central Karlsruhe, with
OpenStreetMap as the second source. What they found:

- The Dutch feed publishes its lorry-park table in DATEX II v2 — inside a
  generic publication's extension, because v2 has no parking publication — and
  the status of the same records in DATEX II v3.
- That status names each site's state in DATEX II v3.2's
  `parkingSiteStatus` (spaces available, almost full, full at the entrance,
  full, unknown), an enumeration later versions dropped; the crosswalk keeps
  it, so the status becomes a `parking.status` reading.
- The status computes occupied spaces from a capacity it does not always
  have, and publishes the difference even when it is negative (−1 261 lorries),
  so an impossible count gives no reading rather than a repaired one.
- Autobahn GmbH's `verkehr.autobahn.de` publishes no licence or terms, so its
  lorry parks are `NOASSERTION` and withheld from the public scope. The same
  rest areas come licensed and live from MobiData BW, which relays Toll
  Collect's lorry-parking service, so the parking catalogue takes them from
  there.
- A lorry park's security rating comes in two incompatible schemes: ESPORG
  grades bronze to platinum, the EU LABEL project rates 1 to 5. The rating
  keeps its scheme's own word instead of being converted, and what the site
  actually does (lighting, fences, patrols) is a separate list.
- The charge-point database relays a national register, so each record names
  its upstream publisher, and its static rows carry a status that is not an
  OCPI one (`STATIC`). A register entry says the charge point exists, not what
  it is doing, so it gives no status reading at all.
- DATEX II and OCPI spell the same plugs differently (`iec62196T2` vs
  `IEC_62196_T2`), and DATEX carries Tesla twice, by market and by plug.
- OCPI has no state for a car plugged in but not charging, and no code for
  megawatt charging, so those two emit nothing rather than a wrong code.
- Italy prices the same grade twice, self service and attended, and sells
  methane by the kilogram while everything else goes by the litre — which is
  why a price hangs on a product, not on a station.
- Spain publishes every grade of every station with an empty string for what
  it does not sell, so a complete list makes a missing product known-absent.
  It also prices grades no European vocabulary had: methanol, ammonia, E25 and
  renewable petrol.
- The Austrian feed answers only per queried area and includes what look like
  the operators' private mail addresses; a business contact is the only
  contact the model carries.
- The motorway operator publishes lorry-park capacities only inside German
  prose lines, under a title its own front end failed to fill.
- In Karlsruhe the charge-point database publishes no site names, so position
  is the only evidence and just one pair is close enough to link; two of its
  sites sit equally near one OSM node, and neither is linked.

The charging catalogue's full payloads, parsed and sealed against the
registry on 2026-10-06, add:

- The charge-point database holds about 268,000 EVSEs, of which about 118,000
  carry a live state; the rest are register entries. Its status feed is read
  filtered to the live states, which is what a reading can come from anyway.
- The Bundesnetzagentur register (116,443 charging devices, 209,136 charge
  points) seals whole and gives no reading: `In Betrieb` and `In Wartung` are
  register states, which set the lifecycle.
- The French consolidation writes 224,488 rows for 167,214 distinct charge
  points: a charge point repeats under its station once per declaration, and
  21,548 rows claim a charge point for a second station. The first claim is
  kept and the others are counted as rejected rather than moving the charge
  point. 119 rows have no charge-point id (`Non concerné`).
- Lithuania's OCPI server cuts a page before it drops the unpublished
  locations, so a page comes back shorter than its limit long before the end;
  the feed reads the register in one request.

## Road network, roadside and borders

The remaining feature kinds of the model are registered by the roads module
and three more definition-only packages:

- `@openconditions/model-roads` adds the road network's fixed things:
  `structure` (bridge, tunnel, overpass, underpass, culvert, gantry) with every
  clearance a register publishes and how it was obtained (measured, calculated,
  signed, design), `rail_crossing`, `blackspot`, `emergency_phone`,
  `lane_control_gantry`; the winter registers `mountain_pass` and
  `chain_control_zone` with `pass.status` and `winter.chain_level`;
  `travel_time_route` with `traffic.travel_time`; and tolls: `toll_point`,
  `toll_section` (one per priced entry and exit), their `toll_lane`
  components, the `toll` offer and the dynamic `toll.price`.
- `@openconditions/model-facilities` registers the `facilities` domain:
  `rest_area` and `weigh_station`, and `facility.open_status`, which any
  feature kind with the kernel's `operated_site` trait carries (toll points and
  border crossings too).
- `@openconditions/model-border` registers the `border` domain:
  `border_crossing`, its queues as `lane_group` components (mode, programme,
  direction, and vehicle class where a source splits queues that way), and
  `border.wait` with the structured `border_wait` result.
- `@openconditions/model-maritime` registers the `maritime` domain:
  `ferry_route` with its `ferry_leg` components, `ferry_terminal`,
  `ferry.status` and `ferry.vehicle_space` (per sailing, named by its
  departure), and the `fare` offer.

Routing reads restrictions from situations only, so a structure's limits are
turned into standing restrictions by `structureRestrictions`: one
`restriction.dimension` situation per limit, with `basis: "structural"` and
the structure in `details.structureRef` and `provenance.derivedFrom`, and a
`closure` when the structure is closed. A signed height is the legal limit
(`meaning: "maximum_permitted"`); where nothing is signed the calculated
height, which already holds the publisher's margin, or else the lowest
measured one is what physically fits (`meaning: "physical_limit"`). A load
posting whose weight the source does not publish becomes an `unsupported`
effect with the issue `value_not_published`: withheld from routing, kept as
evidence.

A service area is one `rest_area` whose car park, filling station and
charging site are features of their own kinds, each `part_of` it; a feature
is part of at most one other, and `partOfTree` builds the tree of a snapshot
and reports parts whose parent is missing and features on a cycle. Recurring
closures are `SeasonalWindow`s (`MM-DD` to `MM-DD`, wrapping the new year),
and `inSeasonalWindow` tests a local date against one.

Two modules may map one value of a shared kernel vocabulary from different
source enumerations: parking occupancy and travel times both report a DATEX
`trend`, keyed `parkingOccupancyTrend:increasing` and
`travelTimeTrendType:increasing`. The registry pools their codes, still
rejects one code naming two values, and `valueTargetCode` takes the
enumeration to emit.

### Road network and roadside fit checks

`network-fit.test.ts` maps the US National Bridge Inventory, the FRA crossing
inventory, the Norwegian road database (height restrictions, tunnel bores,
level crossings, toll stations, mountain passes), Irish collision-rate
sections, APRR motorway tariffs, WSDOT toll rates and pass reports, Caltrans
chain controls and travel times, and a Norwegian DATEX II travel-time
publication. `roadside-fit.test.ts` maps CBP and CBSA border waits, Norwegian
rest areas, weigh stations, ferry connections and quays, Ontario rest areas
and ferries, Iowa weigh scales and Washington State Ferries sailing space.
What they found:

- DATEX II classifies no road structure (its `structureType` is a parking
  place's), and models no toll plaza, weigh station or ferry as an object; the
  registers of those are national, so their mapping lives with each parser.
- A clearance comes in up to five values for one opening: the Norwegian
  database measures the left edge, the middle and the right edge, calculates a
  height with a safety margin and signs a lower one (4.58, 4.3 and 4.0 m in
  one tunnel). The US inventory measures, in metres, and says nothing about
  the posted weight of a bridge it lists as posted.
- Both level-crossing registers list grade-separated crossings too, and the US
  one keeps closed crossings (about 45 %); a passive crossing is one without
  gates, lights, bells or signals, since no field says so.
- Blackspot registers count accidents by their worst outcome, never the
  people hurt, and state their rates and bands in their own words.
- The Norwegian toll register keeps one reference rate per vehicle group, with
  rush-hour windows but no days, and some stations name their hour rule
  without its hour; a French motorway prices every pair of stations in five
  classes defined by height, weight and axles; a US express lane that is not
  being priced reports 0 with .NET's minimum date, which is unknown, not free.
- Caltrans mixes chain levels with operations (escorts, truck holds,
  closures) in one status, dates it when it last changed, gives travel-time
  routes placeholder coordinates and times in seconds its documentation calls
  minutes.
- Neither border agency publishes coordinates or a stable id for CBSA's
  offices; CBP's "no delay" still carries up to eight minutes, and its zone
  labels are wrong for ports outside the eastern zones, so the port's own zone
  dates a wait.
- The Norwegian database publishes every rest-area space count, zeros
  included, and the amenities partly as child objects; Ontario's rest-area
  list holds its truck inspection stations and states a site open whose
  comment says it closed.
- Washington State Ferries reports space and cancellations per departing
  terminal and departure, naming no route.

## Alerts and hazards

`@openconditions/model-hazards` registers the `hazards` domain: warnings of
every kind, weather warnings included, and hazard events from authorities and
satellites. A road that a flood closes stays a `roads` situation, which the
flood causes.

- `alert` is a warning issued as a CAP message. One situation holds one
  message in all its languages: the texts that differ per language, the CAP
  `event` among them, are `Text`, and info blocks that differ in more than
  language become situations of one `groupId`. `details.cap` keeps the
  message as CAP states it: its enumerations snake-cased into the `cap_*`
  vocabularies, its event codes and parameters verbatim. The warning is in
  force from `effective` (CAP's default: `sent`) until `expires`. The event it
  warns of begins at `onset`, which may lie after the message expires, so the
  onset stays in the details, and a warning of an event not yet begun is a
  `forecast`. CAP severity is the declared label (Extreme → critical, Severe →
  major), and an update or cancellation `update_of`s or `cancels` the
  messages it references.
- An alert's type is the hazard it warns of: `wind`, `thunderstorm`, `rain`,
  `snow_ice`, `fog`, `heat`, `cold`, `coastal`, `marine`, `tropical_cyclone`,
  `flood`, `fire`, `avalanche`, `dust`, `air_quality`, `drought`,
  `geophysical`, `hazmat`, `civil`, `missing_person`, `health`, `utility`,
  `transport`, `public_service`, `administrative`, or `other`.
  `capClassification` reads it from the publishers' event lists, most specific
  first: DWD's `II` codes, the Canadian profile's events, the NWS VTEC
  phenomenon, the MeteoAlarm awareness type, then SAME. Every code of every
  list is mapped or explicitly names no hazard.
- `natural_hazard` is the hazard event itself: a wildfire's perimeter, its
  burnt area or a prescribed burn, a smoke plume by density, a flood, a
  landslide, an avalanche, an earthquake with its magnitude and depth, volcanic
  ash, a dust storm. Where a register states where flooding is possible or
  expected without issuing CAP, the flood carries that as the situation's
  certainty.
- `fire.frp` and `fire.brightness` observe one satellite fire pixel at its
  location; a negative reading is refused.
- `capReferences`, `capPolygon` and `capCircle` read CAP's own encodings. CAP
  writes `lat,lon` where GeoJSON writes `[lon, lat]`. A circle becomes a
  derived polygon, split at the antimeridian.

### Alerts and hazards fit checks

`alerts-fit.test.ts` maps CAP messages of the US National Weather Service,
Deutscher Wetterdienst, Environment and Climate Change Canada and, through
MeteoAlarm, Météo-France, MET Norway and AEMET. `hazards-fit.test.ts` maps
NASA FIRMS fire pixels, NIFC fire perimeters, EFFIS burnt areas, NOAA HMS
smoke, Environment Agency flood warnings and USGS earthquakes. What they
found:

- CAP's `expires` ends the message, not the hazard: NWS reissues a watch
  before the heat it warns of begins, so a watch can expire before its onset.
  A MeteoAlarm all-clear expires the moment it takes effect and names no area.
- DWD sends every warning in eight languages in one message and translates
  the event name itself. It cuts the islands and lakes out of a coastal
  district with `EXCLUDE_POLYGON` geocodes, and leaves the German instruction
  empty where the translations carry one.
- NWS sends a generic SAME code (`SVS`) on the update of a tornado warning, so
  the VTEC phenomenon decides; its zone warnings name zones and no polygon.
  DWD reuses seven of its codes for the advance information of the same
  hazard.
- ECCC files fog under the Canadian profile's catch-all `other`, whose 0.4
  list has no fog, so a Canadian fog advisory stays `alert.other` with its
  event text.
- Météo-France and AEMET locate their warnings only by NUTS and EMMA codes,
  and publishers spell the MeteoAlarm awareness type differently, so its
  number is the code.
- USGS puts the hypocentre's depth in kilometres where GeoJSON puts an
  altitude. EFFIS and the Environment Agency write times without a zone (UTC
  and UK local time), HMS as day of the year. NIFC lists prescribed burns among
  its wildfire perimeters and measures in acres.
- FIRMS grades VIIRS confidence in words and MODIS confidence as a
  percentage.

## Crowd and federation

Crowd reports and federation work on every class of the model. What the
crowd may report, and for how long, is part of the registry: a situation
kind or a property with crowd rules takes reports, anything else does not.

- A report claim is what a reporter signs. A situation claim says something
  is happening here: a kind, a registered type and subtype, the geometry and
  how precisely it is known, optionally a severity level from 1 to 5, kernel
  effects, the kind's details and text. An observation claim says this is the
  value now: a property, a feature (and component) or a place, the result as
  the property's observations carry it, and where the reporter stood. A
  sub-claim confirms, negates or flags any record, named by class, id and,
  for a feature, component key.
- Landing turns a claim into a crowd record. The record's id hashes the
  reporter's key with the claim's nonce, so a replay lands the same id and
  the id never reveals the key; the key itself stays in `provenance.reporter`
  and is stripped at every egress. A report lives for its kind's or
  property's lifetime (`freshness.expiresAt`), which confirmations extend up
  to the kind's maximum; the lifetime is never content, so the evidence, not
  a revision, decides when it ends. A report is refused when it was made
  more than a day ago, so long ago that it had already expired, or ahead of
  the server clock. An observation lands on the canonical feature and
  component, and only from a reporter who stood within the property's reach
  of it (300 m by default), measured to where it stands even when only a
  restricted source holds it. Where the reporter stood is checked and not
  kept: a reading of a feature no public source holds sits at the centre of
  the reporter's kilometre area cell, at low resolution.
- Agreement is what corroborates a report and what resolves it externally. A
  situation agrees with another record of the same kind and type that was in
  effect when the report was made and lies within the kind's match distance
  (250 m by default, measured to the nearest part of its geometry). An
  observation agrees with an authoritative reading of the same series that
  was in force when the report was made — a status or a price holds until it
  changes — or arrived within the report's lifetime, with an equal result or
  one within the property's tolerance (a fuel price: a cent). A disagreeing
  reading resolves nothing; the feed may be what is wrong.
- Fusion picks the value a canonical subject shows for one property. Among
  rows in effect now, a fresh row beats a stale one, then the higher tier
  wins (authoritative, operator, aggregator, community, then crowd rows by
  their evidence: externally resolved, corroborated, self-reported), then the
  later reading. A crowd report never overrides a fresh authoritative status;
  it surfaces when the feed is stale or silent, and a negated report, or one
  past its `freshness.expiresAt`, never fuses. The `@fused` row is credited to the winning publisher
  with the most restrictive rights of every source that published the same
  value, lists them in `derivedFrom` and `mergedSources`, and stays on demand
  when one of them is. Whether a candidate is public is decided by
  `isPublicCandidate` (`packages/storage/src/fused-rows.ts`), which also
  states how crowd candidates are judged. When every contributor is public the
  `@fused` row is flagged `fused_public`. Otherwise the fusion of the public
  candidates alone, if any, is kept beside it as the `@fused-public` row. The
  public scope reads the flagged row, the operator the `@fused` row. Neither
  ever enters history, the federation or the archive.
- Components of linked features correspond only by their kind's identity: a
  shared authoritative id (an EVSE's eMI3 id; an OCPI connector id within the
  corresponding EVSE) or, where sources publish no id, equal identity fields
  (a fuel product's grade, service, price level and vehicle scope; a parking
  area's vehicle type and user group). The canonical feature carries every
  member's components: the survivor's keep their keys, the rest join as
  `<source id>/<key>`.
- An instance advertises every schema its registry runs as
  `<key>@<major>.<minor>` (`kernel@1.0`, `situation/incident@1.2`); the actor
  document takes them from the registry, never from configuration. Two
  instances exchange what both run at one major. A record of a kind the
  receiver does not run, or runs at another major, is skipped; fields a
  newer minor added are dropped; an effect kind or an `affects` selector the
  receiver does not know rejects the record rather than losing what it says.
- The outbox carries model records with their class, kind, domain and
  property, and the subscriber filter selects on them as well as on box,
  privacy class, age and evidence. Federation is always public scope: only
  records whose own and upstream licences are public leave, whatever the
  subscriber asks. On-demand answers, fused rows and every record of a
  restricted source never leave the instance, extras only where the source
  opted in, and a reporter's key never. A received record keeps its id, instance and
  evidence and gains a receipt in its origin chain; a peer sends its own
  records only.
- The archive writes one GeoParquet file per class: the records a peer could
  receive that are still current, crowd records only once corroborated,
  records whose licence is not public out, a source's extras only where it opted in, each
  with its class's promoted columns and the record as JSON.

### Crowd fit check

`crowd-fit.test.ts` lands every road category of the OpenMapX report dialog
on the production registry and judges reports against real records: an A5
ramp closure from Autobahn GmbH, Karlsruhe charge points from the MobiData BW
charge-point database, and MINETUR fuel prices. What it found:

- MobiData BW publishes one row per upstream source of a car park, so its
  location id names a row, not an operator's OCPI location. Read as
  `ocpi:location`, two rows of one car park would never link; it is the
  aggregator's `provider` id.
- The Bundesnetzagentur register and the live feed relayed from chargecloud
  describe the same ten charge points with ids that share nothing — the
  register files its own `BNETZA*` numbers in the EVSE id field — so the
  canonical car park has twenty charge points, ten with a live status.
- An operator's status holds from when it changed: EnBW's out-of-order
  status was eleven hours old when it confirmed a driver's report.

## Environment fit check

Before the kernel hardened, three real environmental records were mapped onto
it with a test-only `environment` module (`environment-fit.test.ts`; the
domain itself stays unregistered until OpenConditions ingests such data):
a PEGELONLINE river gauge (Köln), a Sensor.Community citizen node, and an ECCC
AQHI station. All three fit Feature, Component and Observation. What they
changed:

- A linear reference names any linear feature — `LinearRef.ref` ("RHEIN",
  km 688), not a road-only `roadRef`.
- Component kinds are registry-contributed like feature kinds: a citizen node
  is a feature with one `sensor` component per device, and its readings are
  keyed by component and property.
- Sources get a tier (`source_tier`: `authoritative`, `operator`,
  `aggregator`, `community`) and fusion ranks by it, so a citizen network sits
  below an authority. A citizen node never links to an official station
  (different kind), so the tier matters where two publishers carry the same
  asset.
- Qualifiers that identify a series are required: an air-quality index without
  its `scale` is rejected, so a Canadian AQHI never shares a series with a
  European EAQI; concentrations (`air.pm2_5`) and indices (`air.index`) are
  separate properties and never fuse.
- Home-sited sensors are published at coarsened positions
  (`fuzziness: "low_res"`); spatial identity linking will require exact
  positions on both sides.
- Source timestamps without a zone designator (Sensor.Community) are rejected
  until ingest adds one.

## Registry reference

<!-- generated:registry:start -->

Kernel version `1.0`; modules: `kernel`, `roads`, `weather`, `vehicles`, `parking`, `charging`, `fuel`, `facilities`, `border`, `maritime`, `hazards`.

### Domains

| domain       | description                                                                                                                                            |
| ------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `roads`      | Traffic information about the road network: incidents, works, closures, restrictions and conditions, even when weather-caused.                         |
| `weather`    | Measured and forecast meteorological and road-weather data. Warnings are hazards.                                                                      |
| `vehicles`   | Service vehicles an authority operates and tracks: maintenance and response fleets.                                                                    |
| `parking`    | Where vehicles can be parked, and how full it is.                                                                                                      |
| `charging`   | Where an electric vehicle can charge, and whether a point is free.                                                                                     |
| `fuel`       | Where fuel is sold, and what it costs.                                                                                                                 |
| `facilities` | Roadside facilities for drivers and vehicles: rest areas, service areas, weigh stations.                                                               |
| `border`     | Land border crossings and how long their queues are.                                                                                                   |
| `maritime`   | Ferries that carry road traffic: routes, terminals, sailings.                                                                                          |
| `hazards`    | Warnings and hazard events from authorities and satellites, typically area-based: CAP alerts of every category, wildfires, smoke, floods, earthquakes. |

### Crowd reports

| reported                    | lives  | confirmations keep it up to | corroborate / negate | agrees with a record |
| --------------------------- | ------ | --------------------------- | -------------------- | -------------------- |
| `incident`                  | 30 min | 4 h                         | 2 / 2                | within 250 m         |
| `incident.breakdown`        | 15 min | 2 h                         | 2 / 2                | within 250 m         |
| `incident.vehicle_hazard`   | 15 min | 2 h                         | 2 / 2                | within 250 m         |
| `incident.obstruction`      | 15 min | 2 h                         | 2 / 2                | within 250 m         |
| `roadworks`                 | 7 d    | 30 d                        | 2 / 2                | within 250 m         |
| `closure`                   | 4 h    | 1 d                         | 2 / 2                | within 250 m         |
| `weather_condition`         | 15 min | 2 h                         | 2 / 2                | within 250 m         |
| `road_condition`            | 1 h    | 6 h                         | 2 / 2                | within 250 m         |
| `road_hazard`               | 15 min | 2 h                         | 2 / 2                | within 250 m         |
| `equipment_fault`           | 2 h    | 12 h                        | 2 / 2                | within 250 m         |
| `congestion`                | 5 min  | 1 h                         | 2 / 2                | within 250 m         |
| `other`                     | 15 min | 2 h                         | 2 / 2                | within 250 m         |
| `parking.status`            | 30 min | 3 h                         | 2 / 2                | equal result         |
| `charging.evse_status`      | 4 h    | 2 d                         | 2 / 2                | equal result         |
| `charging.connector_status` | 4 h    | 2 d                         | 2 / 2                | equal result         |
| `fuel.price`                | 3 h    | 12 h                        | 2 / 2                | within ±0.01         |
| `fuel.product_available`    | 12 h   | 2 d                         | 2 / 2                | equal result         |
| `facility.open_status`      | 2 h    | 12 h                        | 2 / 2                | equal result         |

### Component identity

| component      | same component when                                                           |
| -------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `parking_area` | equal `vehicleType`, `userGroup`                                              |
| `evse`         | a shared `emi3:evse` or `oicp:evse` or `ocpi:evse` or `datex:refill_point` id |
| `connector`    | a shared `ocpi:connector` id within the same parent                           |
| `fuel_product` | equal `grade`, `service`, `priceLevel`, `vehicleScope`                        |

### Effects

| effect             | version | description                                                                                                                                                         |
| ------------------ | ------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `closure`          | 1.0     | The road element is closed. Individual lanes or the hard shoulder closed = lane_restriction, never a closure.                                                       |
| `lane_restriction` | 1.0     | Lane-level impact (WZDx VehicleImpact, snake_cased).                                                                                                                |
| `speed_limit`      | 1.0     | A (temporary) speed limit in km/h.                                                                                                                                  |
| `delay`            | 1.0     | Expected delay, queue and level of service.                                                                                                                         |
| `access`           | 1.0     | An access rule for the vehicles in `applicability`.                                                                                                                 |
| `dimension_limit`  | 1.1     | A vehicle dimension limit, in the dimension's canonical unit: the legal maximum, or where no legal limit is published, what physically fits (a measured clearance). |
| `hazmat`           | 1.0     | Dangerous-goods restriction. ADR tunnel category A means no restriction and is never an effect.                                                                     |
| `detour`           | 1.0     | A signed or described diversion.                                                                                                                                    |
| `contraflow`       | 1.0     | Traffic runs against the normal direction on part of the carriageway.                                                                                               |
| `advisory`         | 1.0     | Advice without a typed rule.                                                                                                                                        |
| `unsupported`      | 1.0     | Carrier for a source restriction the parser recognised but could not type; always normalization unsupported with issues.                                            |

### Situation selectors

| selector   | version | description                                                       |
| ---------- | ------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `features` | 1.0     | Features (or, with componentKey, components) a situation affects. |

### Structured result schemas

| schema             | version | description                                                   |
| ------------------ | ------- | ------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `vms_display`      | 1.0     | What a sign face shows: text pages, pictograms, lane signals. |
| `camera_image`     | 1.0     | A camera's current image or stream, per view.                 |
| `vehicle_position` | 1.0     | Where a vehicle is and what it is doing.                      |
| `border_wait`      | 1.0     | How long one border queue is, and whether it moves.           |

### Change kinds

| change kind             | classes                   | description                                                                                 |
| ----------------------- | ------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `classification_change` | situation, feature, offer | Kind, type, subtype, causes, temporality, planning or certainty changed.                    |
| `severity_change`       | situation                 | The severity label, level or its source changed.                                            |
| `validity_change`       | situation, offer          | Declared start, end, status, periods or exceptions changed.                                 |
| `effects_change`        | situation                 | An effect was added, removed or changed.                                                    |
| `geometry_change`       | situation, feature, offer | The published geometry changed.                                                             |
| `location_change`       | situation, feature, offer | A location reference other than the geometry changed (roads, direction, lanes, codes).      |
| `text_change`           | situation, feature, offer | Headline, description, instruction, comments or names changed.                              |
| `details_change`        | situation, feature        | The kind's details changed.                                                                 |
| `lifecycle_change`      | feature                   | A feature's lifecycle changed.                                                              |
| `components_change`     | feature                   | A component was added, removed or changed.                                                  |
| `attributes_change`     | feature, offer            | Operator, owner, publisher, opening hours, access, amenities, images or energy mix changed. |
| `price_change`          | offer                     | An offer's prices, currency, tariff type, scope or applicability changed.                   |
| `references_change`     | situation, feature, offer | External ids, relations, the offer subject, affected features or the group changed.         |
| `source_change`         | situation, feature, offer | The source's record version, attribution or allow-listed extras changed.                    |
| `lanes_change`          | situation                 | The lanes closed or open changed.                                                           |
